January 1, 1995


Dear Sacred Harp Singer's and Friends:
You will be getting your 1994 minutes of singings and the 1995 directory
of singings that will be held this years soon. I hope you will find the minute
book holpful in locating a singing to attend this year
In 1994 we had four more singings called off, one convention was changed
to one day, and one convention has no place to go for the 1995 session as
of this date.
After much thought and prayer: ! have decided to resign as secretary of
the Sacred Harp Hinutes begining January 1, 1995. 1 have enjoyed working
with the minutes; alonq with my late husband for thirty years. Walter and
haye put a lot of hours of work in the minutes over these years.  hope all
the work as been of some help to you.
will still attend, and support as many of the the singings as can, as my
work permits me to.


Nora Parker

No matter where we go to sing Sacred Harp, in the North or the South,
19


among new singers or people from families that have been singing roi
generations - we often consider what it is that draws us together, what
attracts us more and more to this experience, and we think, well, it't the
pleasure of singing, yes, and it's the praise of our Creator, certainly, and
it's also that unique sound we make together, but it's always something
more and there's a particular word that describes it. lt's that we are a
community and all the important things that word implies. We are a
community in space and time extending all over this county and beyond its
borders, and stretching back in time, too, to include our beloved singing
friends who have left us. Community is the way we give each other the
roses while we Live. The memorial lesson is one way we affirm and even
celebrate that sense of community. It's an old traditon that becomes more
meaningful to each of us with each passing year." After Ted spoke these
words, Daryl Mayberry led song on page 32b for deceased. The Lacy
family, Henry Johnson, Preston Crider, E. C. Bowen, Curtis McLendon,
Robert Aldridge, Carlton Wood, and many loved ones closer to home.
Patrina Patti led a special memorial from page 66 for Reverend Hubert
Davis, who was a faithful Chicago singers. Phil Trier led his own
arrangement of Shall We Gather at The River for George Armstrong. Ted
Johnson led song on page 70 for the sick and shut-ins. The memorial was
closed with a prayer.

third year that the 1991 Revision of The Sacred Harp has been used and it
was an excellent singing. The morning session was called to order by Dr.
William J. Reynolds, who led song on page 59. The morning prayer was led
by Bruce Coates. After opening comments Dr. Reynolds recognized the
other officers, Vice Chairman, Tim Reynolds; Secretary Dr. Timothy J.
Studstill; Arrainging Committee, Dr. Richard Mauney. The following
leaders were called: Tim Reynolds, 34b; Bruce Coates, 535; Barbara

Morris, 277; Tim Reynolds, 200; Hugh McGraw, 312b; Kelly Beard, 475.

PACIFIC NORTHWEST CONVENTION
Seattle, Washington
February 27, 1994
The third annual session of the Pacific Northwest Sacred Harp Singing
Convention began on Saturday February 26, at the University Friends
Meeting House in Seattle. Terry Wootten conducted a very well-attended
(92 students) singing school. The singing on Sunday, Februay 27, at the
Greenwood Masonic Lodge in Seattle, was called to order at 9:30 a.m. by
Kate Moore leading song on page 65. The morning prayer was led by
Chaplain, Mary Stueban. Leaders: John Kellermann (OR), 64; David Hough,,
40; Darlene Simpson-Brown, 178; Katharine O'Hara, 310. The class then
went into a business session and officers were elected as follows:
Chairman, Karen Willard; Vice Chairman, Bruce Rowland; Secretary,
Darlene Simpson -Brown; Arranging Committtee, David Hough; Memorial
Committee, Connie Karduck, Melanie Hauff, Craig Brandis; Resolutions
Committee, Keith Willard, Reed Schilbach; Locating Committee, Bruce
Rowland. Leaders: Anne Huckins, 107; Reed Schilbach, 61; Karen Willard
led "Washington“; Ken Cofield, 324; Ali Corbin, 300; Marcia Stedman, 180;
Jean Murphy (OR), 380; Ginny Landgraf (CA), led "Bourbon".
RECESS.
The class was called together and polled: There were 36 attending from
Washington, 12 from Oregon, 12, Californina, 4 Illinois, 2 ldaho, 2
Colorado, 2 Minnesota, 2 Alabama, 1 Wisconsin, 1 Gerogia, 1 Vermont.
Leaders: Sandy Hill (C)), 203; Paula Ralston, (CA), 245; Jennie Willard
(MN), 224; Jerry Schrieber (CA), 434; Terry Wootten (AL), 441; Jeannie
Schaffer (CO), 198; Connie Karduck, (IL), 456; Keith Willard (MN), 211;
Alice Sharp (CA), 47; Jerilyn Schumacher (IL), 200; John Seaton (IL),
217; Delbert Long (CA), 551b; Carla Smith (CA), 89; Karen Willard, 326.
RECESS.
Karen Willard reassembled the class leading song on page 345b. Leaders:
Karen Stingle (OR), 85; Elka Schumann (VT), 567; Janet Herman (CA),
440. At this time Connie Karduck conducted the Memorial lesson. She
explained how the openness of Sacred Harp music to the inevitability of
death, trials, and illness in the human condition had transformed her own
attitudes, and how the community of Sacred Harp singers, and especially
the memorial lesson, has enabled her to face these realities. After
speaking of how the names read during the lesson have over the years
changed from unknow strangers to names of much loved friends, Melanie
Hauff led 83 and Craig Brandis led 122 for the following deceased. Norman
Rosser, Henry Mahnke, Ted Kembro, Anna Kimbro, Janice Kmbro Wagoner,
Bonnie Baldwin, Waldo Nicodemus, Ed Siem, Niles Newton, Leonard Lacy,
Noah and Margie Lacy, Preston Crider, Carlton Woods, Henry Johnson,
Runie Glover, Robert Aldridge, E.C. Bowen, Lucille Enright, Humbert Davis,
Neal Summers, William Stafford, Bo Simmons, Arthur Piantadosi, Vera
Lichtenberg, John Scott, Helmuth Schumacher, Sandy Stewart, Bandy
Shilts, and Tony Sendrow. Karen Willard told how she had never
34


personally met Runie Glover, but had come to love her through listening to
her Voice on the many Sacred Harp recordings, seeing her picture in the
hewsletters, reading about her hospitality and hearing the many stories of
her- especilly her unique way of leading HELP ME TO SING. For her Karen
led 375. Jerry Schrieber led 475 for the sick and shut-in.. John Andrist,
Marie Aldridge, Dolly Hudgins, Lora Cargo, Mary Ellen Fairbanks, Barbara
Gates, Catherine Cessna and Jenny Strickler. The Chaplain, Mary Stueban
closed the memorial lesson with prayer. Leaders: Judy Mincey (GA);
Shane Wootten (AL), 163; Jack Lofton, 344; Marly Stueben led "Vermon".
DISMISSED FOR LUNCH.
The afternoon session reconvened with Bruce Rowland leading song on
page 498. Leaders: Gary Lee Johnson, 146; Martha Johnson, 532; Alison
Wells, 209; John Schaffer, 313b; Susan Helf, 99; Stephen OLeary, 81;
Melanie Hauff, 218; Kathy Vlach, 63; Jan Ketelle, 304; Hardin King, 454;
Mary Rose O'Leary, 340; Marcia Herivel, 268; Audrey Karabinus, 58;
Glenn Salisbury, 553; Craig Brandis, 84; Jon Singer 117; Bruce Rowland,

Hill, 143; Jerilyn Schumacher, 361; Shane Wootten, 171; Clara Smith,

512. The business session was held: The Finance committee reported a
surplus of funds after deducting all the expenses of the 1994 convention,
which will be used for the 1995 convention. The Locating Committee
reported that they are looking for a site near Portland, Oregon It was
voted that the 1995 Pacific Nortwest Convention will be held in the greater
metropolitan area of Portland, Oregon. The Resolutions Committee thanked
the hardworking crew in the kitchen: Johnny Jeans, Barbara Willard, and
Allen Stueben; the friendly faces at the registration tables both day: John
Carson, Marianne Costello, Kathy Vlach, Lynn Scroggins, Kathaine O'Hara,
and Anna Huckins; our childcare provider: Pam Mortrud, Jane Mortrud,
Sarah Kraybill, and Jacob McCann; the set up and clean up crew: Ken
Hallock, David Hough, Dana Waters and Glen Salisbury; and the friendly
efficiency of our housing co-ordinator for all of our out-of-town guests,
Ali Corbin. Special mention must be made of the extra assistance provided
by Darlene Simpson-Brown in driving around one of our organizers who
came down with serious car trouble on the very eve of the convention, and
special thanks to Susan Helf who brought in much needed extra lamps when
the singing room turned out to be too dark. A very special thanks you must
also go to Terry Wootten who came all the way from Ider, Alabama to
share his love and knowledge of Sacred Harp with us. Karen Willard and
Bruce Rowland led song on page 62 as the closing song and all who wished
took the Parting Hand. The convention was dismissed with prayer by Hary
Stueben.
Chairman, Karen Willard; Vice Chairman, Bruce Rowland; Secretary
Darlene Simpson-Brown.

WINSTON COUNTY COURTHOUSE MEMORI AL SINGING
Winston County, Alabama
Saturday, February 26, 1994
The first session of the Sacred Harp Memorial Singing was held in
memory of those who have gone on, who started the first singings held in
Double Springs in 1923, and the ones that have kept it going all these
years. Some names of those who help support the singing were: Godsey,
Adams, Keeton, Overton, Denson, Faught ,Snoddy, Shaddix, Odom, Harper,
Martin, Norris, Painter, Creel, Mattox, Moon, Fannin, Watts and more.
The first singing was held in the Courthouse, it was later moved to the

School House where it remained until 1971 when the school burned. After
this the singing is held at Shady Grove Primitive Baptist Church on the
fourth Sunday in September.

POTOMAC RIVER CONVENTION
Washington, DC.
April 15-17 1994
The fifth annual Potomac River Convention began on the evening of
Friday, April 15, with a singing school at St. Paul's Episcopal Church,
Bailey's Crossroads, VA. It was taught by Hugh McGraw and Richard
DeLong and drew over 100 singers many of whom had not sung shape-note
music before. The regular singing sessions were held at Hearst Hall,
National Cathedral School, Washington, DC.
SATURDAY, APRIL 6
The class was called to order by Ella Wilcox leading song on page 66.
Miriam Kilmer offered the opening prayer. A motion was made to go into a
business session, in which the following persons were elected and
appointed: Chairman, Tim Slattery; Vice Chairman, Peter Pate; Secretary,
Clare Chapin; Chaplains, Miriam Kilmer and Mandu Brady; Arranging
Committee, Peter Pate, Tom Tucker and Michele Biery; Finance
Committee, Steven Sabol; Resolutions Committee, George Seiler and Donna
Abrahams; Memorial Committee, Amanda Denson and Kelly Macklin.

Tucker, 504; Gerry Hoffman, 148; Lois Hurt, 661; Ann Ballard, 213; Tim
Ericksen, 416; Elise Meyer-Bothling, 77b; Beth Mayfield, 40; Blake

Tim Slattery called the class together leading song on page 56. Leaders:

Swetnam, "Bethlehem Carol" (His own lyrics set to Cooper book, page 393
Joan Aldridge, 189; Lois Hurt, 79; Richard DeLong, 507. The Memorial
lesson follwed: Amanda Denson opened with the following remarks: "No
one ever crosses jour path by accident. God has brought all of us together
61


todag. It's exciting, rewarding, and fulfilling. Some have the view that
Sacred Harp is folk music. It doesn't matter what you think it is; Sacred
Harp is what it is. It is a form of worship and a form of praise. It doesn't
matter what you believe, this is what it is. Consider the young man who
sang Sacred Harp only in the North for ten years who recently went to the
Antioch singing in Alabama and wrote of his feelings on the Internet; that
man felt the spirit of the Lord at that singing. My great Unce Seab (
Seaborn Denson) was the musical editor for the 1911 James Revision, and
his wife my great Aunt Sidney (Sidney Burdette Denson) put all those Bible
verses under the titles of all the songs. The Spirit of the Lord - almost
every page is inflamed with the Word of God.
"What you believe about God is the most important thing about you. It
colors everything you say and do. Inside every person is a place that only
God can fill, and Sacred Harp is a means of doing this. Sacred Harp brings
out the best in all of us. Sacred Harp Singers act nobly. Sacred Harp
Singers love, and really love each other. Every time we love someone, we
step closer to God. My Aunt Ruthy (Ruth Denson Edwards) once gave a
memorial lesson for the Alabama decessed at Holly Spings, Bremen,
Georgia. She said death is just a natural as living; you just have to do one
before the other. She also said we are all on the same road, some of us just
got started before the others. Decisions - life is made of decisions. God
has provide everithing we need. We just have to have the faith to make the
decisions to accept His blessings.
"And His mercy endures from generation to generation and His
righteousness unto children's children. God delights to use families. Here
today look at the Beasleys, Aldridges, McGraws, Watts, Godseys, DeLongs
and my own family. These are all Sacred Harp families of long standing.
Music and the love of music are a gift from God. The song on page 312 top,
verse 4, saus "Let music charm me last on earth, and greet me first in
heaven." Then Amanda Denson led 260. Rosealtha Taylor led 426 for the
following deceased from Georgia and Alabama: Ashley Ballinger, Henry
Kerr, Runie Glover, Robert Aldridge, Cora Lee Livingston, Levada Gilmore,
Bennie Keeton, E. C. Bowen, Evelyn Dillashaw, Dana Jackson, Margie and
Noah Lacy. Dan Brittain led 446 for the following deceased for the
Northeast: Richard Ely, Jr. (CT), Margaret Hailey (NY), Ellen Osen (NY),
Gabriel and Janine Nadeau (RI), Joseph Sutula (CT), Sigrid Nagle (NJ).
Seorge Seiler led 122 for the following deceaded: Eberhard Meyer-Bothling
(Germany) Mike Jorgenson (CA), and Helmuth Schrmacher (IL). Richard
DeLong led 420 for the following deceased: Glenn Diggs (Va. father of
Glenna Centa, who was in attendance), Bishop Marlin Ballard (MD), and
John Hack (TN). Barbara Briggs led 340 for the sick and shut-ins: Klaus
Meyer-Bothling (MD), Jim Posey (VA), Marty Cook (MD), Connie Mattson
(FL), John and Catherine DeNys (IL), Robin Kogelschatz (NY), David Lowe

NORTH CAROLINA SACRED HARP CONVENTION
Raleigh, North Carolina
May 1, 1994
The annual North Carolina Sacred Harp Convention began with two
events on Saturday, April 30. a composers workshop from 2-5 p.m. at
John Feddersen's home in Raliegh, and a singing school and dessert social at
Watts Street Baptist Church in Durham from 7-9 p.m.
On Sunday, the all day singing began at 9:30 A.M at the Glen Eden
Community Center in North Raleigh. The class thanked Lynda Hambourger
and the space Committee for finding this great place to sing - live room
with no carpet (!) ,plenty of space, natural lighting, and trees all around.
They also expressed appreciation to the Pinecone Peidmont Council of
Traditional Music for paying the day's rent for us.
Officers from the previous year's convention not being present yet, G.
L. Waldrep volunteered to open with Old Hundred, page 49. Henry Bizzell
then offered an opening prayer. When Nancey Newell, 1993 chairman,
arrived, she led the election of new officers. By acclamation, G.C. Waldrep
was elected Chairman, Vice Chairman, Tim Gee, Secretary, Erin Newton;
Chaplain, Henry Bizzell. Leaders: Erin Newton, 546; Lynda Hambourger,

GARDEN STATE SACRED HARP SINGING CONVENTION
Montclair Friends Meetinghouse, Upper Montclair, New Jersey
May 13-14, 1994
On Friday evening, May 13, the second Garden state Sacred Harp
Singing Convention was called to order by Jean Seiler who led 34b and
welcomed all present. Bill Holt led the opening prayer. The class organized
by electing the following officers: Co-Chairmen Jean and George Seiler;
Secretary, Guy Bankes; Chaplain, Bill Holt; Reception Committee, Richard
Schmeidler; Arranging Committee, Dennis O'Brien, Bob Parr and Dottie
Hurley; Memorial Committee, Eric Bean, Blake Morris, and Martha Jane
Husick; Finance Committee, Dan Hertzler and Paula McGray; Housing
Committee, Jim Brittain; Food Committee, Martha Jane Husick, Sally
Findley and Margaret Crowl; Child Care Coordinator, Sally Findley;
Resolutions Committee, Tim Slattery and Miriam Kilmer. Jean Seiler gave
77


a brief review of the shape-note system and led song on page 49. Leaders:
George Seiler, 49b; Guy Bankes, 481; Dan Hertzler, 107; Margaret Crowl,
178; Ginnie Ely, 475; Blake Morris, 67; Diomie Johnson and Eric Bean, 39
Bob Parr, 200; Ruth Fansler, 159; Mary Williams Greene led 28b and
extended greetings and love from her granfather Deweg Williams, a well
-known mernber of the Wiregrass Singers, The Alabama group that is
preserving the Colored Sacred Harp tradition. Karen Snowberg, 569.
RECESS.
Ihe class was called to order by George Seiler leading song on page 45.
He then introduced Ginnie Ely of North Reading, Massachusetts, who
proceeded to teach a singing school on the basics of Sacred Harp singing
with special attention to leading. She led the class in two exercises
encouraging participants to practice the shapes both with and without
written music. She led 358. She discussed the relationship of time
signatures to tempo, and, as examples, led 35, 294 and 389. Noting that
singers must feel the beat to stay together she requested the class to all
beat time with their hands for the remaining tunes. She led 313b, 171,
68b, 131b. She discussed the role of the front row singers, then went on
to techniques of leading, giving all an oportunity to practice by having each
section lead a tune: basses, 84; leads, 228; altos, 155; trebles, 40. Ms.
Ely concluded by leading 454. George Seiler announced that Dan Brittain.
who could not be present, had written " Garden State" for this convention.
Ginnie Ely was asked to lead it. After an expression of gratitude by Mr.
Seiler for her singing school, the class was dismissed following a closing
prayer by Bill Holt.

85


Jerry Enright brought the class together leading song on page 77.
Leaders: Val Eng (MN), 313; Debbie Hall (OH), 34b; David Ivey, (AL), 53;
Richard Green, Rebecca Green (KS), Virgil Phillips, 147; Jim Parsons (MN),
133; Lou Cotney (AL), 189; Terry Wootten (AL), 201b; Kathleen Thro,
448; Steven Sabol (MD), 567; Jeff Sheppard, (AL), 29b; Sandy Hill (CO),
344; Buell Cobb (AL), 131b; Lee Steinmetz 565; Jewel Wootten, (AL), 29;
J.C. Hardin (AL), 97; Ann Steel (MS), 30; Jim Page (W1), 193b; Fred Todt
(OH), 480; Ginnie Ely (MA), 391; Seth Houston (VT), 107; Barb Swetman
(NY), 127; Michael Hieber (OH), 276. The memorial lesson was held at this
time, with Stephen O'Leary addressing the class with the following
message: "At a California Convention a few years back, our chaplain, Jim
Freidrich, said some words in his invocation that have stuck with me and
grown upon me: he called the hollow square in which we sing "an image and
a foretaste of the union for which we were made." "Our friends whom we
mourn and remember today have gone before us to that union. We have the
foretaste, but they have discovered for themselves the true banquet; we
anticipate, we live in hope from meal to meal, but they know that the hill
of Zion yields a thousand sacred sweets.
But l'd like to say a few words about our relationship with those who
have gone before, and the task that we face of building that union which
never dies. For we are called to be the builders, carpenters, and masons of
that heavenly city of which we siny.
In the book of Revelation, Chapter 21, we read that this city is laid out
like a square, as long as it is wide. The wall was made of jasper, and the
city of golds, as pure as glass. The foundations of the city walls were
decorated with every kind of precious stone. We read that there is no
temple in the city, becsuse the Lord God Almight and the Lamb are its
temple. And the book tells us that the gates of the city are never to be
shut, for there will never be night there: For the glory of God gives it
light, and the Lamb is its lamp.
My friends, I speak to you in the spirit to tell you this: While we live,
we will never be closer to that heavenly city, the city that lies
foursquare, than we are when we come together to sing. And more: we
are charged with the task of construction the New Jerusalem, bringing it
down to earth, building it up.
Let this be our memorial to those whom we mourn today. Let us vow
never to aband on our own work of building; let us sing with all our might
and build the New Jerusalem with our song. For it is a city not made with
hands, but with the human voice. Basses: How firm the foundation you lay
for our city! Tenors: Lift up Zion's walls with your melodies! Trebles:
Raise high the roof beam! Altos: adorn our city with your shining grace!
Let our songs build the city of God as our memorial to our friends who
have gone before us- great builders all, who now can see for themselves
the plan of the Master Architect. For our songs today will fade into
silence, but the city will stand forever, and the union that we build will
never die.


Let the gates our city be always open to all who would join us. For as
long as we keep this tradition alive, our friends will be remembered in the
city that we build with songs-- the city on the Hill. Jan Ketelle then read
the names of the deceased: (Al) Noah and Margie Lacy, Preston Crider,
Runie Glover, Henry Kerr, Estelle Glenn. (GA) Louis Cox, E.C. Bowen, Nina
Johnson, Carlton Wood, Dana Jackson, Evelyn Dillashaw. (IL) Lucille
Enright, Helmuth Schmacher, Rev. Hubert Davis, Rev. Talka Krienscieck,
Norbert Rokusek. (NH) Lilly Mediouni; (CT) Richard O. Ely, Jr.; (MD) Egon
Middleman; (CA) Barbara Goodell; (ME), Dorcas Stevens; (MS) Walter
Robertson; (KS) Jean Freeman Deacy. Gladys Noah, Alene Harrington, Paul
Nussbaum; John Cook, Denis Duginske, Terpola Williams, Rita Smoker;
Helen Housel, Chuck Gabrion, Ye-she Nam-dag. Stephen O'Lear led song on
page 299 and Don Bowen led 494 for the deceased. Becky Browne reminded
the class that sickness covers all kinds of suffering, and that we are
tempted to put up a wall between ourselves and pain, but the wall also
separates us from others too. We need to tear down that wall and
experience and share the pain, and in sharing our suffering, we are healed
spiritually, if not also physically, too. If we put our pain and cares in the
hollow square, there will be healing. She then led song on page 372 for the
sick and shut-ins, who are as follows; Tammy Webster, John Snowbert,
Cleo Hawkins, Sara Cury; Marie Aldridge, Camilla Haase, Baby Andrew
Hall; Winford Lacy, Mae Holt, Pauline Childers, Janet Bell, Ken Taylor, Eva

Jessica Roberts, 186; Janet Herman, 224; Tom Owen, 250; Cora Sweatt,
145b; Bruce Rye, 300. The memorial lesson was held at this time. Becky
Browne then introduced the subject of the memorial lesson by speaking
about the tradition of Sacred Harp. While many people were born in this
tradition, others have joined the tradition from many different
backgrounds. The Sacred Harp fellowhship has always opened its arms to
allow newcomers, not making demands or asking questions but lovingly
receiving and patiently teaching. Mant of those listed had been involved in
the teaching which she had received.
The Sacred Harp tradition speaks of the mortality of humanity. It is in
the lyries of the music of the memorial lesson. It asks us to ponder the
question that lsaae Watts asked, 'Hast thou made mankind in vain:' Are we
simply a collection of molecules brought together by some act of chance?
No, there is a loving God who is watching us and we see it in the music and
the love we show for one another and the love that these have shown to us.
The list of deceased were read as follows; Alabama: Bert Smith, Preston
Crider, Goldie Brothers, Runie Glover, Estelle Glenn, Minerva Harper,
Ashley Ballinger, Noah and Margie Lacy; Bob Gardner, Maudine Wallace

Resolution Committee of the 15th National Sacred Harp Convention would
like first and foremost, to thank God, "from whom all blessing flows,"
"Who hast preserrved us from our birth," especially for the gift of song
and friendship. The fellowship of kindred minds is a foretaste of the
heavenly joys to come. Secondly, we extend many thanks to Trinity United
Methodist Church and all its staff who have been so kind in hosting this
convention. We would like to thank thern in advance for the invitation to
return next year. Special praise should go to Pennie Bosarge for organizing
the dinners and also to all those who cooked, served and cleaned up.
Thirdly, we would like to give thanks for and express our appreciation to
all the officers and committee mernbers, who, each in their proper station,
have worked so hard to make this convention a success. We express our
appreciation as well for their willingness to serve in the coming year
knowing all the effort that is involved in leaading and organizing a
convention such as this. Fourthly, we wish God's blessings on all of those,
both singers, listeners and helpers, who have come from far and near to be
a part of this convention and fellowship. Without you all, it would not have
been a success. Fifthly, we wish to extend a very special thank you to
Gary and Sarah Smith for opening their home to us and providing
abundantly for a fine time of fellowship last evening. Respectfully
submitted, Andrew Albers, Judy Mincey and Linda Thomas. A motion was
made and seconded to accept the report of the committees and close the
business session.

welcome after a morning of singing and as a background for fellowship. To
make the singing of Sacred Harp music our mission and to support Sacred
Harp singings whenever and wherever we can find them. To stay in contact
with each other by phone, mail, contributions to newsletters and by word
of mouth because we care about each other. To Keep in our hearts thoughts
and prayers and to mention in our memorial lessons those suffering and
dying in places remote-the ill, the impoverished, victims of vilence and
disease and of war and plain viciousness and inhumanity; and to remember
we are not to judge other human beings that we should clear our souls of
anger and other undesirable feelings and fill ourselves with love,
happiness, and kindness, the feelings we all experience when we sit
132


shoulder to shoulder and sing these old tunes as loud as we can for God's

from an eighteenth century Quaker Hymn aptly describe the situation in
which we find ourselves today. We are where we ought to be! We are in the
valley of love and delight. Love and Good-will are flowing. There's truly a
sweet, Sweet, Sprit in this place. While this hymn is now reallg ancient,
its message is ageless, tiroeless. The Great composer, Aaron Copeland,
included the hymn in one of his beautiful compositions, The New World
Symphony. The hymn also reminds us that the valley of love and delight is
the valley of the shadow of death as well.

now made sight. As their names are read, stand if you hear the name of one
dear to you. After a moment of silent prayer, the following names were

then spoke for the sick and shut-ins "In just a moment l'll read the list of
those unable to bring their bodies into our square this weekend because of
illness. This is a time not only to remember them and to do what we can by
remembrance and singing to help their healing along, but also in our heart,
to put ourselve in their places; to bring the square to thern. We need to
understand, as Becky Browne said at least year' Alabama State
Convention, that we will be on this list one day. So this moment is one of
letting go of our fear and entering with others into a community of the
brokenness of our bodies, these sweet and and and comfy old coats of flesh
we wear and also into a community of healing, of making whole and holy.
By putting our bodies into the center here to lead, we are pierced by what
flows from the hearts of our friends in the singing and accept a gift of
healing energy. In certain parts of Africa, where singing into the body is
practiced there is no history of viral lllness. So join me for a moment in
mentally placing those ill friends we love into the center of our square this
morning." Stephen then read the sick and shut-ins list. Rey. Cathy Ann
Beatty, Lee Stinmetz, Ellen Crule, Sara Curry, Camilla Hasse, Maurice
164

SOUTH GEORGIA SACRED HARP SINGING CONVENTION
Lebanon Primitive Baptist Church - Near Cordele, Georgia
November 6, 1994
The seventy-fifth session of the South Georgia Singing Convention was
called to order by David Grant, leading song on page 403. The opening
prayer was offered by J. O. Hall. The chairman sorrowfully announced
that due to the death of one of our most faithful and beloved singers, Harry
Dumas, the singing would be an abbreviated version of our usual
convention. He asked that each leader choose only one song. It was agreed
upon that the singing would only continue until lunchtime after which those
attending could adjourn to attend the funeral in Perry. David Grant then
appointed Mary Brownlee and Ann Corbin to the Memorial Committee.
Joyce Harrison, Jon and Kay, Robinson Arranging Committee. Leaders:
Rita Haley, 59; Georgia Corbin, 340; Otis Hester, 47; Marjorie Robinson,
39b; Sharon Hamrick, 385; Bonnie Rewis, 62; Misty Haley, 155; Bob
Corbin, 341; John Garst, 159; Michael Pate, 298; Beth Haley, 354b;
Myrtice Meeks, 358; John Hollingsworth, 496; Bob Meeks, 75; David
Grant, 311; Jon and Kay Robinson, 144; Charles Grant, 100; Raymond
Hamrick, 27; Doris Haley, 565b, Pattie Collins, 490; Joyce Harrison,
236; Jehu Tyson, 45; Timoth Haley, 46; Katie McGuire, 515b; Lou Ann
Turner, 147; David Grant, 329.
A memorial service was held at this time. Ann Cobin led song on page
569b for those who were sick or shut-ins. These shut-ins included; Mrs.
Gertrude Bateman, Carlton Hitchcock and wife Ruth, and Mrs. Runell
Crawtord. Mary Brownlee led for those who have passed away since our
last convention. This list consisted of only two names, Harry Durnas and
Mrs. Berry Wilkinson. We also remembered some others who have been
lost to our group in the last few years including Horace Hamrick and Clinton
Haley. Mary Brownlee led page 479 for all of these. This concluded the
memorial service.
Chairman, Grant called for the business session. Raymond Harmick
Chairman, of th nominating Committee was asked to present a slate of
officers for consideration by the Convention. The following names were
presented: Chairman, Oscar McGuire: Vice, Chairman, Richard Johnson;
Secretary/ treasurere; Mildred Dumas; Assistant Secretary/Treasurer;
Rita Haley; Chaplain, Robert Meeks; These officers were duly elected. The
new officer's terms begin at the close of this convention. The singings for
the coming year were reviewed and changes made (changes appear on the
list of singings page of the minutes) in other general business, Joyce
Harrison called for a resolution to dedicate the entire proceedings to the
memory of Harry Dumas. This was unanimously passed and is reflested
186


in these minutes. The business session was then Closed. Oscar McGuire,
newly elected Chairmain, closed out the convention leading song on page
56. Bob Meeks dismissed the convention with prayer.
Chairman, David Grant; Vice Chairman, Oscar McGuire; Secretary and
Treasurer, Mlildred Dumas; Asst. Secretary and Treasurer, Rita Haley.

Linda Thomas made the presenation. God brings many people into our lives.
Some bring a word of encouragement, some spreads a little sunshine, some
just gives a hug, or simply a little love. When they do these things and
more, we call them friends. Aren't you thankful for firends? l'm glad that
God is a friend thats closer than a brother. He will never leave us or
forsake us. I'm thankful for the joy in him that unspeakable, and peace that
passes understanding. He is our joy and our song. He is our conforter in
sorrow, our strength in every trial and His grace is always sufficient. At
this time we're going to remember our friends who have passed away since
last year. As long as we're here we will miss them. In 2 Samuel 12: When
Davids child died he said,"I shall go to him, but he shall not return to me,"
We're on our way to them. Lets make it a good journey, make good
memories, and live a life others can follow. The decease from Florida: T. H.

ALTAMONT FAIR (TRICOUNTY FAIR GROUNDS)
Near Albany, New York
June 25, 26, 1994
A singing school and singing from the Denson book and Northern
Harmony were held on June 25 and 26 on the grounds of the Altamont Fair.
This is an annual singing held on the fourth Sunday and Saturday before as a
part of the Old Songs Festival of Traditional Music and Dance. The singing
school on Saturday afternoon was taught by Peter Amidon. Each day, the
singing began at 9:30 a.m. All lessons were from the Sacred Harp unless
other wise noted. Songs on Saturday were: 48, 146, 155, 107, 352, 178,
49, 209, 454, 236, 189, 276, 62. Songs on Sunday were: 107, 47, 352;
59, 198, 497, 200, 268, (122 for memorial lesson), 31, 273, 155, 191,
480, 186, 90 (Northern Harmony), 495, 62. Hope to see every one next
year.
Secretary, Linda Delfs.
